In Slice 0 the only mover is migrate(), which\n220\t        RENAMES (byte-preserving), so the current index offsets stay valid for the resolved file. Fully\n221\t        epoch-invalidated, segment-spanning reads land in Slice 1.\"\"\"\n222\t        a = self._active\n223\t        if a.exists():\n224\t            return a\n225\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n226\t        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n227\t        if act is not None:\n228\t            r = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n229\t            if r.exists():\n230\t                return r\n231\t        # PRE-manifest migrate window: the atomic rename has already placed the data at seg-0 but the\n232\t        # manifest is not published yet (so `m is None`). Read it there — otherwise a lock-free reader in\n233\t        # exactly this instant sees a false-EMPTY spine (kill-switch un-halt / nonce-floor regression) and\n234\t        # the indexed read path can hit a bare FileNotFoundError. seg-0 is byte-identical (rename), so the\n235\t        # index built over the vanished file stays valid on it.\n236\t        if m is None:\n237\t            seg0 = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n238\t            if seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists():\n239\t                return seg0\n240\t        return a\n241\t\n242\t    def _segments_in_order(self) -> list[Path]:\n243\t        \"\"\"The segment files to read, in seq order (sealed by first_seq, then the active tail). With no\n244\t        manifest this is the single legacy/active file. Resolved fresh each call so a rotation/migration by\n245\t        another process is seen. RAISES SpineError on a manifest-referenced segment file that is MISSING —\n246\t        a read must never silently yield a short chain (invariant 15: no state-scanner fail-open).\"\"\"\n247\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n248\t        if m is None:\n249\t            t = self._read_target()\n250\t            return [t] if t.exists() else []\n251\t        out: list[Path] = []\n252\t        for seg in m.ordered():\n253\t            p = self._layout.seg_path(seg)\n254\t            if not p.exists():\n255\t                raise SpineError(\n256\t                    f\"spine manifest (generation {m.generation}) references a missing segment: {seg.file}\")\n257\t            out.append(p)\n258\t        return out\n259\t\n260\t    def _seam_tip_for_active(self) -> \"ChainEntry | None\":\n261\t        \"\"\"When the ACTIVE segment is empty, the chain tip its first append must extend so the seam stays\n262\t        contiguous: the prior SEALED segment's real last record (its boundary). Returns None ONLY when the\n263\t        active is the GENESIS segment (first_seq == 0) — a genuinely fresh spine correctly starts at seq 0.\n264\t        For a rotated (first_seq > 0) empty active whose seam boundary cannot be read, it FAILS CLOSED\n265\t        (raises) rather than returning None — because append treats None as 'genesis' and would otherwise\n266\t        fork the chain at seq 0 (invariant 13). The WRITE path must fail closed exactly like the read path.\n267\t        MUST be consulted under the append lock (reads the manifest fresh). No-op in Slices 0-2 (genesis).\"\"\"\n268\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n269\t        if m is None:\n270\t            return None\n271\t        act = m.active()\n272\t        if act is None or act.first_seq == 0:\n273\t            return None                              # genesis: a fresh spine correctly starts at seq 0\n274\t        sealed = m.sealed_in_order()\n275\t        if sealed:\n276\t            entry = self._entry_from_line(self._last_line_of(self._layout.seg_path(sealed[-1])))\n277\t            if entry is not None:\n278\t                return entry\n279\t        raise SpineError(\n280\t            f\"active segment first_seq={act.first_seq} is non-genesis but its seam boundary (the prior \"\n281\t            f\"sealed segment) is unreadable — refusing to fork the chain at seq 0\")\n282\t\n283\t    @contextmanager\n284\t    def _crossproc_lock(self) -> \"Iterator[None]\":\n285\t        \"\"\"Cross-process exclusion on the inode-stable lockfile (invariant 14 / D3). flock binds to the\n286\t        open file DESCRIPTION, not the path, so an os.replace of the manifest/segments cannot move the\n287\t        lock out from under a concurrent writer — which the prior flock-on-the-data-fd could not survive.\n288\t        Best-effort exactly as before (fcntl absent on non-POSIX; a flock failure is swallowed). NOT\n289\t        re-entrant across fds — never nest a second `_crossproc_lock` inside one (a second LOCK_EX on the\n290\t        same file from a different fd self-deadlocks); the seal-swap that runs inside append reuses the\n291\t        already-held lock rather than re-acquiring.\"\"\"\n292\t        if fcntl is None:  # pragma: no cover — non-POSIX\n293\t            yield\n294\t            return\n295\t        self._layout.lockfile_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n296\t        fd = os.open(str(self._layout.lockfile_path), os.O_RDWR | os.O_CREAT, 0o600)\n297\t        try:\n298\t            try:\n299\t                fcntl.flock(fd, fcntl.LOCK_EX)\n300\t            except OSError:  # pragma: no cover\n301\t                pass\n302\t            yield\n303\t        finally:\n304\t            os.close(fd)\n305\t\n306\t    def _complete_orphan_migration_locked(self) -> bool:\n307\t        \"\"\"MUST hold `_crossproc_lock`, and be called only when the manifest is currently ABSENT. If we are\n308\t        in the migrate() crash window — seg-0 present, `spine.jsonl` gone (the rename is atomic + durable),\n309\t        no manifest — finish the interrupted migration by publishing the manifest. Returns True iff it\n310\t        completed one. Called from BOTH __init__'s reconciler AND append's `_refresh_active_under_lock`, so\n311\t        a LIVE appender completes the orphan under the lock instead of re-creating `spine.jsonl` and forking\n312\t        the log (the critical review finding).\"\"\"\n313\t        seg0 = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n314\t        if seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists():\n315\t            write_manifest(self._layout, initial_manifest(SCOPE, active_file=self._layout.segment_rel(0)))\n316\t            _log.warning(\"spine: completed an interrupted migration — published the manifest for %s\", seg0)\n317\t            return True\n318\t        return False\n319\t\n320\t    def _refresh_active_under_lock(self) -> None:\n321\t        \"\"\"MUST hold `_crossproc_lock`. FIRST complete any interrupted migration (so a live appender never\n322\t        resurrects `spine.jsonl`), THEN re-resolve the active segment from the manifest so an appender\n323\t        constructed before a concurrent migrate()/rotation writes to the CURRENT target — never a stale\n324\t        path (fork) or a renamed-away file (resurrection). One manifest read in the steady state.\"\"\"\n325\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n326\t        if m is None and self._complete_orphan_migration_locked():\n327\t            m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n328\t        new_active = self.path\n329\t        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n330\t        if act is not None:\n331\t            new_active = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n332\t        changed = new_active != self._active or (m is not None) != (self._manifest is not None)\n333\t        self._manifest = m                          # keep fresh so the rotation check reuses it (no 2nd read)\n334\t        if changed:\n335\t            self._active = new_active\n336\t            self._invalidate_index()\n337\t            self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n338\t\n339\t    def _invalidate_index(self) -> None:\n340\t        \"\"\"Drops the segment-aware index so the next read rebuilds it against the (possibly changed)\n341\t        segment set.\"\"\"\n342\t        with self._index_lock:\n343\t            self._offsets = {}\n344\t            self._seg_scanned = {}\n345\t            self._index_epoch = None\n346\t            self._max_seq = -1\n347\t\n348\t    def _reconcile_orphan_migration(self) -> None:\n349\t        \"\"\"Close the migrate() crash window at CONSTRUCTION. migrate() does an ATOMIC\n350\t        `os.replace(spine.jsonl → segments/seg-00000000.jsonl)` and THEN publishes the manifest; a crash\n351\t        BETWEEN them leaves seg-0 present, `spine.jsonl` gone, and no manifest — at which point the legacy\n352\t        fallback (active = spine.jsonl) would read the spine as EMPTY and silently hide all history. If we\n353\t        observe exactly that state, finish the (already-durable) migration under the lock. (A live appender\n354\t        does the same via `_refresh_active_under_lock`; a construction-time pass just recovers faster.)\"\"\"\n355\t        if self._manifest is not None:\n356\t            return\n357\t        seg0 = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n358\t        if not (seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists()):\n359\t            return\n360\t        with self._crossproc_lock():\n361\t            if read_manifest(self._layout) is None:          # re-check under the lock\n362\t                self._complete_orphan_migration_locked()\n363\t            self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n364\t\n365\t    def change_token(self) -> tuple:\n366\t        \"\"\"A cheap, rotation-aware epoch token — (manifest generation, active file, size, inode) — that\n367\t        changes on ANY append (size), a migration/rotation (generation + active path/inode), or a reset.\n368\t        THE unified replacement for the size-only 'has the spine changed?' heuristic at every site\n369\t        (invariant 9 / A4) — notably the kill-switch verdict caches in `governor/killswitch.py` and\n370\t        `gesture/session.py`. Because it reads the manifest FRESH and keys on the resolved active segment\n371\t        (not the legacy `store.path`, which a migration renames away), a same-size rotation — or a\n372\t        migration performed by another process — can never serve a stale (un-halting) kill-switch verdict.\"\"\"\n373\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n374\t        gen = m.generation if m is not None else -1\n375\t        active = self.path\n376\t        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n377\t        if act is not None:\n378\t            active = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n379\t        try:\n380\t            st = active.stat()\n381\t            return (gen, str(active), st.st_size, st.st_ino)\n382\t        except OSError:\n383\t            return (gen, str(active), -1, -1)\n384\t\n385\t    def migrate(self) -> bool:\n386\t        \"\"\"Move a legacy single-file spine into the segment layout: rename `spine.jsonl` →\n387\t        `<stem>.segments/seg-00000000.jsonl` (atomic, same filesystem — O(1), no byte copy) and publish a\n388\t        single-active-segment manifest. Idempotent (a no-op once a manifest exists). Returns True iff it\n389\t        migrated. SPLIT-BRAIN SAFETY: run this only after every spine writer is on code that flocks the\n390\t        path-stable lockfile (this Slice-0 code) and re-resolves the active segment under that lock — a\n391\t        stale appender then completes/observes the migration instead of resurrecting `spine.jsonl`. After\n392\t        migration `spine.jsonl` is absent and is NEVER re-created as a data file (all data I/O targets the\n393\t        active segment).\"\"\"\n394\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n395\t            with self._crossproc_lock():\n396\t                if read_manifest(self._layout) is not None:\n397\t                    self._refresh_active_under_lock()\n398\t                    return False\n399\t                self._layout.segments_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n400\t                seg0_abs = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n401\t                if self.path.exists():\n402\t                    os.replace(self.path, seg0_abs)         # atomic rename (same fs); O(1), no copy\n403\t                    fsync_dir(self._layout.spine_dir)       # persist the SOURCE-dir unlink of spine.jsonl\n404\t                else:\n405\t                    seg0_abs.touch()                        # fresh spine: create the empty active segment\n406\t                fsync_dir(self._layout.segments_dir)        # persist the TARGET-dir creation of seg-0\n407\t                write_manifest(self._layout, initial_manifest(SCOPE, active_file=self._layout.segment_rel(0)))\n408\t                self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n409\t                self._active = self._resolve_active_path()\n410\t                self._invalidate_index()\n411\t                self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n412\t                return True\n413\t\n414\t    def reset(self) -> None:\n415\t        \"\"\"Delete EVERY spine artifact for this store — the legacy data file, the manifest, all segments,\n416\t        the trash, and the lockfile — for `sigil ingest --reset` (a full rebuild). Under the cross-process\n417\t        lock so it can't race an append. Idempotent; leaves the store readable (empty) afterward. Replaces\n418\t        the old `SPINE_PATH.unlink()`, which left the manifest + rotated segments behind (stale).\"\"\"\n419\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n420\t            with self._crossproc_lock():\n421\t                # NB: do NOT unlink the lockfile — we hold its flock. flock binds to the INODE, not the\n422\t                # path (invariant 14), so unlinking it would let a concurrent cross-process appender open a\n423\t                # NEW inode at the same path and acquire the flock during our destructive rmtree — losing an\n424\t                # acked record and dangling the (re-published) manifest at a deleted segment. It is a tiny\n425\t                # 0-byte token whose entire purpose is path-stability; keep it so reset() truly excludes\n426\t                # appenders for its whole critical section.\n427\t                for pth in (self._layout.manifest_path, self.path):\n428\t                    try:\n429\t                        pth.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n430\t                    except OSError:\n431\t                        pass\n432\t                for dpath in (self._layout.segments_dir, self._layout.trash_dir):\n433\t                    if dpath.exists():\n434\t                        shutil.rmtree(dpath, ignore_errors=True)\n435\t                self._manifest = None\n436\t                self._active = self._resolve_active_path()\n437\t                self._invalidate_index()\n438\t                self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n439\t\n440\t    def generation(self) -> int:\n441\t        \"\"\"The manifest generation (monotonic rotation epoch); -1 for a legacy (un-migrated) spine.\"\"\"\n442\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n443\t        return m.generation if m is not None else -1\n444\t\n445\t    def segment_info(self) -> list[dict]:\n446\t        \"\"\"A CLI/human summary of the segment set: one dict per segment in seq order. Empty list for a\n447\t        legacy (un-migrated) single-file spine.\"\"\"\n448\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n449\t        if m is None:\n450\t            return []\n451\t        out: list[dict] = []\n452\t        for seg in m.ordered():\n453\t            p = self._layout.seg_path(seg)\n454\t            out.append({\"id\": seg.id, \"codec\": seg.codec, \"sealed\": seg.sealed,\n455\t                        \"first_seq\": seg.first_seq, \"last_seq\": seg.last_seq,\n456\t                        \"bytes\": p.stat().st_size if p.exists() else 0, \"file\": seg.file})\n457\t        return out\n458\t\n459\t    # --- rotation (retain-all seal-swap) ------------------------------------------\n460\t    def _maybe_rotate_locked(self) -> None:\n461\t        \"\"\"MUST hold the flock (called at the end of append, after the ack). Seal the active segment + start\n462\t        a fresh one if it hit a size/record threshold. Only for a MIGRATED store (a legacy single-file spine\n463\t        never auto-rotates — the owner runs `sigil spine migrate` first). Write-then-rotate: the triggering\n464\t        record is already durable in the active before we seal, so a panic append is never blocked by\n465\t        rotation for more than the bounded (O(1)+O(tail)) seal-swap (D1).\"\"\"\n466\t        if not (self._seg_max_bytes or self._seg_max_records):\n467\t            return\n468\t        m = self._manifest                          # kept fresh by _refresh_active_under_lock\n469\t        if m is None:\n470\t            return\n471\t        act = m.active()\n472\t        if act is None:\n473\t            return\n474\t        ap = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n475\t        try:\n476\t            size = ap.stat().st_size\n477\t        except OSError:\n478\t            return\n479\t        tip = self._last.seq if self._last is not None else -1\n480\t        records = (tip - act.first_seq + 1) if tip >= act.first_seq else 0\n481\t        if ((self._seg_max_bytes and size >= self._seg_max_bytes)\n482\t                or (self._seg_max_records and records >= self._seg_max_records)):\n483\t            self._seal_active_locked(m, act, ap)\n484\t\n485\t    def _seal_active_locked(self, m: Manifest, act: Segment, ap: Path) -> bool:\n486\t        \"\"\"MUST hold the flock. Seal the active segment `act` and start a fresh empty active, publishing a\n487\t        new-generation manifest ATOMICALLY (the single commit instant). Retain-all: no record is removed, so\n488\t        the signed head + verify are untouched and entry_count stays absolute. Bounded (O(1)+O(tail)).\n489\t        Ordering is crash-consistent: build the new active durably FIRST, then the manifest swap is the\n490\t        commit — a crash before it leaves the pre-rotation spine intact (the triggering record already\n491\t        durable in the old active); a crash after it leaves the post-rotation set. Returns True if sealed.\"\"\"\n492\t        # R2: seal-time torn-tail truncate (A2). The append-path truncate never revisits a sealed segment,\n493\t        # so this is the ONLY place a just-sealed file is cleaned; usually already clean (we just fsync'd).\n494\t        clean_end, boundary = _last_valid_boundary(ap)\n495\t        if boundary is None:\n496\t            return False                            # empty active — nothing to seal\n497\t        try:\n498\t            pre = ap.stat().st_size\n499\t        except OSError:\n500\t            return False\n501\t        if pre > clean_end:\n502\t            with ap.open(\"r+b\") as f:\n503\t                f.truncate(clean_end)\n504\t                f.flush()\n505\t                os.fsync(f.fileno())\n506\t        seq_hi, boundary_hash = boundary.seq, boundary.entry_hash\n507\t        new_id = act.id + 1\n508\t        new_abs = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(new_id)\n509\t        # R1: GC an orphan next-segment left by a prior CRASHED seal (created but never committed to a\n510\t        # manifest). Safe: it is not in the committed segment set, so it holds no acked record.\n511\t        if new_abs.exists():\n512\t            new_abs.unlink()\n513\t        # R3: create the empty new active durably (it EXISTS before the manifest names it, so a reader that\n514\t        # sees the new manifest always finds the active — no \"active absent\" window).\n515\t        fd = os.open(str(new_abs), os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL | os.O_WRONLY, 0o600)\n516\t        os.fsync(fd)\n517\t        os.close(fd)\n518\t        fsync_dir(self._layout.segments_dir)\n519\t        _maybe_crash(\"seal_after_new_active\")       # crash-fuzz: new active created, manifest NOT yet swapped\n520\t        # R4: publish the new-generation manifest — the ATOMIC COMMIT INSTANT (temp→fsync→replace→dir-fsync).\n521\t        sealed = Segment(id=act.id, file=act.file, codec=act.codec, sealed=True,\n522\t                         first_seq=act.first_seq, last_seq=seq_hi, count=seq_hi - act.first_seq + 1,\n523\t                         first_prev_hash=act.first_prev_hash, boundary_hash=boundary_hash, bytes=clean_end)\n524\t        new_active = Segment(id=new_id, file=self._layout.segment_rel(new_id), codec=\"none\", sealed=False,\n525\t                             first_seq=seq_hi + 1, first_prev_hash=boundary_hash)\n526\t        others = [s for s in m.segments if s.id != act.id]\n527\t        write_manifest(self._layout, Manifest(generation=m.generation + 1, scope=m.scope,\n528\t                                              segments=others + [sealed, new_active]))\n529\t        _maybe_crash(\"seal_after_manifest\")         # crash-fuzz: committed; in-memory state not yet updated\n530\t        self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n531\t        self._active = self._layout.seg_path(new_active)\n532\t        # A seal moves NO bytes (retain-all): the just-sealed segment keeps its path + content, so its\n533\t        # indexed offsets stay valid. Do NOT clear the whole index (that would force an O(whole-spine)\n534\t        # re-scan every ~12000 records); just drop the epoch so the next read reconciles — which finds every\n535\t        # sealed segment already indexed (skip) and only registers the new empty active.\n536\t        with self._index_lock:\n537\t            self._index_epoch = None\n538\t        self._last = boundary                       # tip preserved; the next append seeds from the seam\n539\t        return True\n540\t\n541\t    def rotate(self) -> bool:\n542\t        \"\"\"Force a seal of the active segment now (for `sigil spine rotate` / an opportunistic seal at\n543\t        checkpoint). No-op on a legacy (un-migrated) or empty active. Returns True if it sealed.\"\"\"\n544\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n545\t            with self._crossproc_lock():\n546\t                self._refresh_active_under_lock()\n547\t                m = self._manifest\n548\t                if m is None:\n549\t                    return False\n550\t                act = m.active()\n551\t                if act is None:\n552\t                    return False\n553\t                return self._seal_active_locked(m, act, self._layout.seg_path(act))\n554\t\n555\t    # --- compaction (gzip-on-seal; retain-all — no record removed) -----------------\n556\t    def compact(self) -> int:\n557\t        \"\"\"Compress every SEALED plaintext segment to gzip. Retain-all: no record is removed, and a `.gz` is\n558\t        byte-decompress-identical, so verify()/the signed head are unaffected. Reclaims ~5-8x on JSONL. The\n559\t        heavy gzip runs LOCK-FREE (a sealed segment is immutable — no append is blocked); only the tiny\n560\t        per-segment manifest codec-flip takes the flock. After the flip the superseded plaintext is UNLINKED\n561\t        (immediate reclaim). Readers are protected NOT by lingering the plaintext but by (a) POSIX open-fd\n562\t        survival for a reader mid-scan, and (b) the manifest-reread retry in iter_records/tail/get for a\n563\t        reader that resolved the path but opens it after the unlink. On a non-POSIX FS where unlinking an\n564\t        in-use file fails, the unlink is deferred to the next compact() (no crash). Idempotent. Returns the\n565\t        number compressed.\"\"\"\n566\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n567\t        if m is None:\n568\t            return 0\n569\t        n = 0\n570\t        for seg in m.sealed_in_order():\n571\t            if seg.codec == \"none\" and self._compress_one_sealed(seg.id):\n572\t                n += 1\n573\t        self._remove_superseded_plaintext()          # immediate reclaim + backstop for a crash before this\n574\t        return n\n575\t\n576\t    def _compress_one_sealed(self, seg_id: int) -> bool:\n577\t        \"\"\"P1 (lock-free gzip of the immutable sealed segment) + P2 (brief-locked manifest codec flip).\n578\t        Crash-safe: the .gz is built to a UNIQUE temp (so two concurrent compactors never clobber a shared\n579\t        temp) then atomically renamed; the codec flip is the atomic commit. A crash before the flip leaves an\n580\t        orphan .gz the next run overwrites — the plaintext (still referenced) is never at risk.\"\"\"\n581\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n582\t        seg = next((s for s in (m.segments if m else []) if s.id == seg_id and s.sealed and s.codec == \"none\"), None)\n583\t        if seg is None:\n584\t            return False\n585\t        plain = self._layout.seg_path(seg)\n586\t        if not plain.exists():\n587\t            return False\n588\t        gz_rel = self._layout.segment_rel(seg_id, \"gzip\")\n589\t        gz_abs = self._layout.spine_dir / gz_rel\n590\t        fd, tmp_name = tempfile.mkstemp(dir=str(self._layout.segments_dir),\n591\t                                        prefix=f\".{segment_filename(seg_id)}.\", suffix=\".gz.tmp\")\n592\t        os.close(fd)                                 # unique temp per process — no shared-temp clobber\n593\t        tmp = Path(tmp_name)\n594\t        try:\n595\t            with plain.open(\"rb\") as fin, gzip.open(tmp, \"wb\", compresslevel=6) as fout:\n596\t                shutil.copyfileobj(fin, fout)\n597\t            with open(tmp, \"rb\") as f:\n598\t                os.fsync(f.fileno())\n599\t            os.replace(tmp, gz_abs)\n600\t        except BaseException:\n601\t            try:\n602\t                tmp.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n603\t            except OSError:\n604\t                pass\n605\t            raise\n606\t        fsync_dir(self._layout.segments_dir)\n607\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n608\t            with self._crossproc_lock():\n609\t                m2 = read_manifest(self._layout)\n610\t                if m2 is None:\n611\t                    return False\n612\t                flipped, segs2 = False, []\n613\t                for s in m2.segments:\n614\t                    if s.id == seg_id and s.sealed and s.codec == \"none\":\n615\t                        segs2.append(s.model_copy(update={\"codec\": \"gzip\", \"file\": gz_rel,\n616\t                                                          \"bytes\": gz_abs.stat().st_size}))\n617\t                        flipped = True\n618\t                    else:\n619\t                        segs2.append(s)\n620\t                if not flipped:\n621\t                    return False                     # another process already flipped it\n622\t                write_manifest(self._layout, Manifest(generation=m2.generation + 1, scope=m2.scope, segments=segs2))\n623\t                self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n624\t                with self._index_lock:\n625\t                    self._index_epoch = None         # segment path+codec changed -> reconcile offsets\n626\t        return True\n627\t\n628\t    def _remove_superseded_plaintext(self) -> None:\n629\t        \"\"\"Unlink the plaintext seg-k.jsonl of every segment the manifest now marks GZIP — the superseded\n630\t        copy after a codec flip (or one stranded by a crash between the flip and this call). Immediate disk\n631\t        reclaim. A reader mid-read keeps its POSIX open-fd; a reader that resolved the path re-resolves via\n632\t        the read-path retry. On a non-POSIX FS an in-use unlink raises and is deferred to the next compact().\n633\t        Idempotent.\"\"\"\n634\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n635\t        if m is None:\n636\t            return\n637\t        for seg in m.segments:\n638\t            if seg.codec != \"gzip\":\n639\t                continue\n640\t            plain = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(seg.id, \"none\")\n641\t            try:\n642\t                plain.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n643\t                plain.with_name(plain.name + \".idx\").unlink(missing_ok=True)   # its now-stale Slice-5 sidecar\n644\t            except OSError:                          # in use on a non-POSIX FS -> the next compact() retries\n645\t                pass\n646\t\n647\t    # --- write --------------------------------------------------------------------\n648\t    def append(\n649\t        self, *, kind: str, source: str, actor: str, payload: dict[str, Any],\n650\t        parent_id: int | None = None, supersedes_id: int | None = None,\n651\t        ts: str | None = None,\n652\t    ) -> int:\n653\t        content = {\n654\t            \"scope\": SCOPE, \"kind\": kind, \"source\": source, \"actor\": actor,\n655\t            \"payload\": payload, \"parent_id\": parent_id, \"supersedes_id\": supersedes_id,\n656\t        }\n657\t        cert_digest = digest_payload(content)  # wallclock-free\n658\t        # Serialize the whole read-tip → write so concurrent writers (threaded bridge server, gesture\n659\t        # daemon) can't both fork off a stale tip and break the chain. The in-process RLock is keyed on\n660\t        # the STABLE `self.path` (unchanged), so the check-then-append gate `envelope.consume` builds on\n661\t        # top of it stays atomic across a rotation. Re-read the TRUE tip from disk under the lock.\n662\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n663\t            with self._crossproc_lock():                 # cross-process guard on the path-stable lockfile\n664\t                self._refresh_active_under_lock()        # pick up a concurrent migrate()/rotation; never fork\n665\t                active = self._active\n666\t                # binary append+read: lets us TRUNCATE a torn tail before writing (BLOCK-1 fix). `a+b`\n667\t                # creates the file if absent and — in append mode — every write still lands at EOF.\n668\t                with active.open(\"a+b\") as f:\n669\t                    # BLOCK-1: an interrupted write can leave torn bytes PAST the last valid record. Appending\n670\t                    # after them would MERGE (torn + new) into one unparseable line → the new record is silently\n671\t                    # lost, verify() stays green, and a lost kill-switch panic never halts the mesh. Truncate the\n672\t                    # dead tail back to the last valid record FIRST (this only removes never-committed garbage\n673\t                    # from an interrupted write; committed records are untouched). Evaluated on the ACTIVE\n674\t                    # segment under the lock (invariant 7 — the append target is always the true chain tip).\n675\t                    clean_end, last = _last_valid_boundary(active)\n676\t                    pre_size = os.fstat(f.fileno()).st_size\n677\t                    if pre_size > clean_end:\n678\t                        f.truncate(clean_end)\n679\t                        _log.warning(\"spine: truncated a %d-byte torn tail (interrupted write) before append (%s)\",\n680\t                                     pre_size - clean_end, active)\n681\t                    if last is not None:\n682\t                        entry = append_entry([last], cert_digest)          # extend the active segment's tip\n683\t                    else:\n684\t                        # active is EMPTY: seed from the prior sealed segment's boundary if this is a rotated\n685\t                        # (non-genesis) segment, else start the genesis chain at seq 0. Never build_chain a\n686\t                        # non-genesis segment from GENESIS (that would fork the chain at seq 0 — invariant 13).\n687\t                        seam = self._seam_tip_for_active()\n688\t                        entry = append_entry([seam], cert_digest) if seam is not None else build_chain([cert_digest])[0]\n689\t                    record = {\n690\t                        \"seq\": entry.seq, **content, \"ts\": ts or now_iso(),\n691\t                        \"cert_digest\": cert_digest, \"prev_hash\": entry.prev_hash, \"entry_hash\": entry.entry_hash,\n692\t                    }\n693\t                    line = json.dumps(record, ensure_ascii=False) + \"\\n\"\n694\t                    offset = clean_end                      # after any truncate, EOF == clean_end (where the line lands)\n695\t                    f.write(line.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n696\t                    f.flush()\n697\t                    os.fsync(f.fileno())                    # FIX 3: an ack'd append is durable across a crash\n698\t                    _maybe_crash(\"append_after_fsync\")      # crash-fuzz: record durable, not yet acked/sealed\n699\t                    # FIX 1: keep the index current with NO re-scan when the ACTIVE segment is already\n700\t                    # indexed exactly up to this record's offset. If another PROCESS appended in between,\n701\t                    # `_seg_scanned[active] < offset` and we skip — the next read's `_ensure_index` extends\n702\t                    # the active over the gap bytes AND this one. `_index_epoch` is intentionally left stale\n703\t                    # so that next read refreshes it (its extend then finds nothing to do — O(1)).\n704\t                    with self._index_lock:\n705\t                        active_str = str(active)\n706\t                        if self._seg_scanned.get(active_str) == offset:\n707\t                            self._offsets[entry.seq] = (active_str, offset)\n708\t                            if entry.seq > self._max_seq:\n709\t                                self._max_seq = entry.seq\n710\t                            self._seg_scanned[active_str] = offset + len(line.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n711\t                self._last = entry\n712\t                # write-then-rotate: the record is durable (fsync'd) above BEFORE any seal, so a panic\n713\t                # append is never blocked by rotation for more than the bounded seal-swap (D1). No-op unless\n714\t                # a migrated active hit a threshold.\n715\t                self._maybe_rotate_locked()\n716\t        return entry.seq\n717\t\n718\t    # --- read ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n719\t    def _open_segment(self, p: Path):\n720\t        \"\"\"Open a segment for a forward byte read, transparently decompressing a gzip-sealed segment. For a\n721\t        `.gz` the index stores DECOMPRESSED byte offsets, which is exactly what `gzip.GzipFile.seek()` (used\n722\t        by get()) consumes, so the offset semantics match. The active segment is always plaintext.\"\"\"\n723\t        if p.suffix == \".gz\":\n724\t            return gzip.open(p, \"rb\")\n725\t        return p.open(\"rb\")\n726\t\n727\t    def iter_records(self, *, since_seq: int = -1) -> Iterator[SpineRecord]:\n728\t        \"\"\"Records with seq > `since_seq`, in seq order, ACROSS ALL SEGMENTS (sealed segments then the\n729\t        active) as ONE contiguous chain, so every full-scanning consumer keeps seeing the whole log. For\n730\t        since_seq >= 0 the index seeks straight to the first wanted line in its owning segment\n731\t        (O(records-returned), not O(spine)); a full read (since_seq < 0) streams every segment from the\n732\t        first. A line that fails to parse or lacks required keys is SKIPPED (a torn active tail can't crash\n733\t        the read; a torn MIDDLE line becomes a seq gap that verify() fails on). RAISES SpineError if the\n734\t        manifest references a segment that cannot be read — a read must NEVER silently yield a short chain\n735\t        (invariant 15: no state-scanner fail-open).\"\"\"\n736\t        yielded_upto = since_seq\n737\t        for _attempt in range(6):                        # D2 reader-grace: tolerate a few compaction moves\n738\t            segs = self._segments_in_order()             # RAISES on a genuinely missing referenced segment\n739\t            if not segs:\n740\t                return\n741\t            start_i, start_off = self._start_locus(yielded_upto, segs)\n742\t            try:\n743\t                for i in range(start_i, len(segs)):\n744\t                    p = segs[i]\n745\t                    with self._open_segment(p) as f:\n746\t                        if i == start_i and start_off:\n747\t                            f.seek(start_off)\n748\t                        for raw in f:\n749\t                            if not raw.strip():\n750\t                                continue\n751\t                            try:\n752\t                                rec = SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(raw))\n753\t                            except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n754\t                                _log.warning(\"spine: skipping malformed line during iter_records (%s)\", p)\n755\t                                continue\n756\t                            if rec.seq > yielded_upto:\n757\t                                yield rec\n758\t                                yielded_upto = rec.seq\n759\t                return                                    # completed cleanly\n760\t            except FileNotFoundError:\n761\t                # a concurrent compaction moved a plaintext segment to trash AFTER we resolved it — re-read\n762\t                # the manifest (now pointing at the .gz) and continue from where we left off. Retain-all\n763\t                # means the same seqs are still covered, so no record is skipped or repeated.\n764\t                _log.debug(\"spine: a segment moved during read (compaction); re-resolving from seq %d\", yielded_upto)\n765\t                continue\n766\t        raise SpineError(\"spine segments kept moving during read (compaction churn) — giving up\")\n767\t\n768\t    def get(self, seq: int) -> SpineRecord | None:\n769\t        \"\"\"A single record by seq via the segment-aware index (resolves the OWNING segment + offset),\n770\t        byte-identical to a full scan. O(1) for a plaintext segment; for a gzip-sealed segment the offset is\n771\t        a DECOMPRESSED offset, so `gzip.seek()` decompresses up to it (bounded by the seal threshold — not a\n772\t        full-spine scan). Falls back to a bounded forward scan (spanning segments) when the seq is not\n773\t        indexed (beyond the tip, or a corrupt line was skipped).\"\"\"\n774\t        if seq < 0:\n775\t            return None\n776\t        self._ensure_index()\n777\t        with self._index_lock:\n778\t            loc = self._offsets.get(seq)\n779\t        if loc is not None:\n780\t            path_str, off = loc\n781\t            try:\n782\t                with self._open_segment(Path(path_str)) as f:\n783\t                    f.seek(off)\n784\t                    raw = f.readline()\n785\t                rec = SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(raw))\n786\t                if rec.seq == seq:\n787\t                    return rec\n788\t            except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n789\t                pass                                     # stale/moved offset — fall through to the scan\n790\t        for r in self.iter_records(since_seq=seq - 1):\n791\t            if r.seq == seq:\n792\t                return r\n793\t            if r.seq > seq:\n794\t                break\n795\t        return None\n796\t\n797\t    def tail(self, n: int) -> list[SpineRecord]:\n798\t        \"\"\"The last `n` records, walking segments backward from the ACTIVE across the seam (O(n) bytes,\n799\t        NOT O(spine)) — a bounded RECENT-window read on a large spine. Fewer than `n` if the spine is\n800\t        shorter. FIXES B3: the pre-Slice-1 version read only the active file, so right after a rotation it\n801\t        returned < n. NOTE (unchanged): a bounded window collapses a rapid recent replay flood but does NOT\n802\t        bound AGGREGATE replay bloat — pair `tail()`-based dedup with a record-time freshness gate.\"\"\"\n803\t        if n <= 0:\n804\t            return []\n805\t        for _attempt in range(6):                        # D2 reader-grace: a concurrent compaction may\n806\t            try:                                         # unlink a plaintext between resolve and read\n807\t                recs: list[SpineRecord] = []\n808\t                for p in reversed(self._segments_in_order()):\n809\t                    recs = self._read_segment_tail(p, n - len(recs)) + recs\n810\t                    if len(recs) >= n:\n811\t                        break\n812\t                return recs[-n:]\n813\t            except FileNotFoundError:\n814\t                _log.debug(\"spine: a segment moved during tail() (compaction); re-resolving\")\n815\t                continue\n816\t        raise SpineError(\"spine segments kept moving during tail() (compaction churn) — giving up\")\n817\t\n818\t    def _read_segment_tail(self, p: Path, k: int) -> list[SpineRecord]:\n819\t        \"\"\"The last `k` records of a single segment. Plaintext: seek-from-end (O(k) bytes). Gzip: decompress\n820\t        (bounded by the seal threshold) and take the last k lines — no random access into a gz stream.\n821\t        Opening a segment that was resolved from the manifest but has since VANISHED (a concurrent\n822\t        compaction unlinked the plaintext) RAISES FileNotFoundError so tail() re-resolves — NOT a silent []\n823\t        (which would drop a whole segment from the window and hole the chain; the arm replay-dedup gate\n824\t        depends on a complete window).\"\"\"\n825\t        if k <= 0:\n826\t            return []\n827\t        if p.suffix == \".gz\":\n828\t            with gzip.open(p, \"rb\") as f:                # raises FileNotFoundError if it vanished -> tail() retries\n829\t                lines = [x for x in f.read().split(b\"\\n\") if x.strip()]\n830\t        else:\n831\t            with p.open(\"rb\") as f:                      # raises FileNotFoundError if it vanished -> tail() retries\n832\t                f.seek(0, os.SEEK_END)\n833\t                pos = f.tell()\n834\t                buf = b\"\"\n835\t                while pos > 0 and buf.count(b\"\\n\") <= k:\n836\t                    step = min(65536, pos)\n837\t                    pos -= step\n838\t                    f.seek(pos)\n839\t                    buf = f.read(step) + buf\n840\t            lines = [x for x in buf.split(b\"\\n\") if x.strip()]\n841\t        out: list[SpineRecord] = []\n842\t        for ln in lines[-k:]:\n843\t            try:\n844\t                out.append(SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(ln)))\n845\t            except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n846\t                continue\n847\t        return out\n848\t\n849\t    @property\n850\t    def next_seq(self) -> int:\n851\t        return (self._last.seq + 1) if self._last else 0\n852\t\n853\t    def count(self) -> int:\n854\t        return sum(1 for _ in self.iter_records())\n855\t\n856\t    # --- integrity ----------------------------------------------------------------\n857\t    def entries(self) -> list[ChainEntry]:\n858\t        return [\n859\t            ChainEntry(seq=r.seq, prev_hash=r.prev_hash, cert_digest=r.cert_digest, entry_hash=r.entry_hash)\n860\t            for r in self.iter_records()\n861\t        ]\n862\t\n863\t    def verify(self) -> tuple[bool, str]:\n864\t        \"\"\"Two-layer UNKEYED integrity: (1) BINDING — each record's payload still hashes to its\n865\t        stored cert_digest (catches silent payload edits); (2) CHAIN — the entries link cleanly\n866\t        (catches delete/reorder/entry tamper). This proves internal CONSISTENCY, not authenticity:\n867\t        a naive payload edit fails (1), and a mid-chain digest edit cascades an entry_hash/prev_hash\n868\t        break caught by (2) — BUT a writer who recomputes cert_digest+entry_hash for the tip (no\n869\t        successor to cascade into) or forward-cascades a fork produces a self-consistent chain that\n870\t        passes here. Resistance to a recompute-capable writer is the owner-SIGNED head's job\n871\t        (`checkpoint.verify_checkpoint`, Ed25519 + monotonic last_seq). Use this for corruption/\n872\t        naive-tamper detection; use the signed head for tamper-EVIDENCE.\"\"\"\n873\t        entries: list[ChainEntry] = []\n874\t        for r in self.iter_records():\n875\t            content = {\n876\t                \"scope\": r.scope, \"kind\": r.kind, \"source\": r.source, \"actor\": r.actor,\n877\t                \"payload\": r.payload, \"parent_id\": r.parent_id, \"supersedes_id\": r.supersedes_id,\n878\t            }\n879\t            if digest_payload(content) != r.cert_digest:\n880\t                return False, f\"binding break at seq {r.seq}: payload does not match cert_digest (record tampered)\"\n881\t            entries.append(ChainEntry(seq=r.seq, prev_hash=r.prev_hash, cert_digest=r.cert_digest, entry_hash=r.entry_hash))\n882\t        return verify_chain(entries)\n883\t\n884\t    @staticmethod\n885\t    def _entry_from_line(line: str | None) -> \"ChainEntry | None\":\n886\t        if not line:\n887\t            return None\n888\t        d = json.loads(line)\n889\t        return ChainEntry(seq=d[\"seq\"], prev_hash=d[\"prev_hash\"], cert_digest=d[\"cert_digest\"],\n890\t                          entry_hash=d[\"entry_hash\"])\n891\t\n892\t    def _last_line_of(self, p: Path) -> str | None:\n893\t        \"\"\"The last VALID record line of a segment, codec-aware. Plaintext: seek-from-end (O(tail)). Gzip:\n894\t        decompress (bounded by the seal threshold) and take the last well-formed line — used only for the\n895\t        seam boundary of an already-compressed sealed segment (a rare empty-active edge).\"\"\"\n896\t        if not p.exists():\n897\t            return None\n898\t        if p.suffix == \".gz\":\n899\t            last = None\n900\t            with gzip.open(p, \"rt\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n901\t                for line in f:\n902\t                    s = line.rstrip(\"\\n\")\n903\t                    if not s.strip():\n904\t                        continue\n905\t                    try:\n906\t                        d = json.loads(s)\n907\t                    except ValueError:\n908\t                        continue\n909\t                    if all(k in d for k in _REQUIRED_KEYS):\n910\t                        last = s\n911\t            return last\n912\t        return _last_nonempty_line(p)\n913\t\n914\t    def _read_last_entry(self) -> ChainEntry | None:\n915\t        \"\"\"The chain tip = the last valid record of the ACTIVE segment. If the active is EMPTY (a freshly\n916\t        rotated segment before its first append), the tip is the last SEALED segment's real last record\n917\t        (the seam boundary), so next_seq never regresses / reuses a seq across a rotation (invariant 13).\n918\t        Skips a torn/garbage tail, so a crash mid-write can't block a restart.\"\"\"\n919\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n920\t        if m is None:\n921\t            t = self._read_target()\n922\t            return self._entry_from_line(_last_nonempty_line(t) if t.exists() else None)\n923\t        act = m.active()\n924\t        if act is not None:\n925\t            ap = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n926\t            line = _last_nonempty_line(ap) if ap.exists() else None   # active is always plaintext\n927\t            if line:\n928\t                return self._entry_from_line(line)\n929\t        for seg in reversed(m.sealed_in_order()):        # active empty/absent -> last sealed segment's tail\n930\t            line = self._last_line_of(self._layout.seg_path(seg))     # may be gz-compressed\n931\t            if line:\n932\t                return self._entry_from_line(line)\n933\t        return None\n934\t\n935\t    # --- segment-aware seq -> (segment, byte-offset) index ------------------------\n936\t    def _start_locus(self, since_seq: int, segs: list[Path]) -> tuple[int, int]:\n937\t        \"\"\"(starting segment index in `segs`, byte offset within it) so a forward read yields exactly\n938\t        seq > since_seq. A full read (since_seq < 0) → (0, 0). Otherwise the index seeks to seq\n939\t        (since_seq + 1) in its owning segment; if that seq is beyond the tip, return a past-the-end index\n940\t        so nothing is yielded; if not resolvable (below the min, or a skipped/corrupt gap), fall back to a\n941\t        safe full span from segment 0.\"\"\"\n942\t        if since_seq < 0:\n943\t            return (0, 0)\n944\t        self._ensure_index()\n945\t        want = since_seq + 1\n946\t        with self._index_lock:\n947\t            loc = self._offsets.get(want)\n948\t            max_seq = self._max_seq\n949\t        if loc is not None:\n950\t            path_str, off = loc\n951\t            for i, p in enumerate(segs):\n952\t                if str(p) == path_str:\n953\t                    return (i, off)\n954\t            return (0, 0)                           # segment set changed under us → safe full span\n955\t        if want > max_seq:\n956\t            return (len(segs), 0)                   # nothing beyond the tip → yield nothing\n957\t        return (0, 0)                               # below min / gap → safe full span\n958\t\n959\t    def _ensure_index(self) -> None:\n960\t        \"\"\"Build/reconcile the segment-aware index lazily, keyed on the epoch (`change_token`). On an epoch\n961\t        change (an append grew the active, or a rotation/migration changed the segment set) reconcile each\n962\t        segment: the ACTIVE extends over the new bytes (or re-scans if it shrank in place — a truncation);\n963\t        a SEALED segment is immutable and indexed exactly once. Thread-safe under `_index_lock`.\"\"\"\n964\t        tok = self.change_token()\n965\t        segs = self._segments_in_order()            # RAISES on a missing referenced segment, so get() (which\n966\t        #                                             calls _ensure_index) fails CLOSED too — never a\n967\t        #                                             fail-OPEN point read from a surviving segment while the\n968\t        #                                             chain is provably truncated (invariant 15).\n969\t        active_str = str(segs[-1]) if segs else None\n970\t        with self._index_lock:\n971\t            if tok == self._index_epoch:\n972\t                return\n973\t            for p in segs:\n974\t                ps = str(p)\n975\t                try:\n976\t                    size = p.stat().st_size\n977\t                except OSError:\n978\t                    continue\n979\t                scanned = self._seg_scanned.get(ps, 0)\n980\t                if ps == active_str:\n981\t                    if size > scanned:\n982\t                        self._scan_segment(ps, p, scanned, size)     # extend the active over new bytes\n983\t                    elif size < scanned:                             # active shrank in place (truncation)\n984\t                        self._purge_segment(ps)\n985\t                        self._scan_segment(ps, p, 0, size)\n986\t                elif scanned < size:                                 # sealed: immutable → index once\n987\t                    self._index_sealed(ps, p, size)\n988\t            self._index_epoch = tok\n989\t\n990\t    def _index_sealed(self, ps: str, p: Path, size: int) -> None:\n991\t        \"\"\"Index a SEALED (immutable) segment. First try its persisted `.idx` sidecar (Slice 5) so a cold\n992\t        start LOADS the seq→offset map instead of re-scanning up to a full segment (a gz segment would\n993\t        otherwise decompress fully). On a miss/stale/corrupt sidecar, scan the segment and write a fresh one\n994\t        (best-effort). The sidecar is a pure CACHE derived from the segment bytes — never a tamper input —\n995\t        validated against the segment's current size on load, so a changed segment regenerates it. MUST hold\n996\t        `_index_lock`.\"\"\"\n997\t        if self._load_sidecar(ps, p):\n998\t            return\n999\t        self._scan_segment(ps, p, self._seg_scanned.get(ps, 0), size)\n1000\t        self._write_sidecar(ps, p)\n1001\t\n1002\t    def _load_sidecar(self, ps: str, p: Path) -> bool:\n1003\t        \"\"\"Populate `_offsets` for a sealed segment from its `.idx` sidecar iff it exists and matches the\n1004\t        segment's current byte size. Returns True if loaded. A stale/corrupt/absent sidecar → False (the\n1005\t        caller scans + rewrites).\"\"\"\n1006\t        idx = p.with_name(p.name + \".idx\")\n1007\t        try:\n1008\t            if not idx.exists():\n1009\t                return False\n1010\t            d = json.loads(idx.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n1011\t            if d.get(\"v\") != 1 or d.get(\"seg_bytes\") != p.stat().st_size:\n1012\t                return False                                 # different segment bytes → the sidecar is stale\n1013\t            first, offsets = int(d[\"first_seq\"]), d[\"offsets\"]\n1014\t            if not isinstance(offsets, list):\n1015\t                return False\n1016\t            # Cheap validity spot-check: the first offset must point to the record with seq == first_seq.\n1017\t            # Catches a wholesale-wrong / shifted / tampered sidecar without re-scanning (one read; a gz\n1018\t            # decompresses cheaply to its start). The sidecar only ACCELERATES a seek — verify()/count()/the\n1019\t            # security state-scanners full-scan WITHOUT the index, and get() re-checks rec.seq==seq per\n1020\t            # lookup, so a bad sidecar can at worst slow a read, never make a floor under-count.\n1021\t            if offsets:\n1022\t                with self._open_segment(p) as f:\n1023\t                    f.seek(int(offsets[0]))\n1024\t                    rec = SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(f.readline()))\n1025\t                    if rec.seq != first:\n1026\t                        return False\n1027\t        except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n1028\t            return False\n1029\t        for i, off in enumerate(offsets):\n1030\t            seq = first + i\n1031\t            self._offsets[seq] = (ps, int(off))\n1032\t            if seq > self._max_seq:\n1033\t                self._max_seq = seq\n1034\t        self._seg_scanned[ps] = p.stat().st_size             # mark fully indexed → the reconcile skips it\n1035\t        return True\n1036\t\n1037\t    def _write_sidecar(self, ps: str, p: Path) -> None:\n1038\t        \"\"\"Persist a sealed segment's seq→offset map as `<segment>.idx` (best-effort, atomic). Offsets are\n1039\t        collected from `_offsets` in seq order — a segment holds a CONTIGUOUS seq range, so only the offset\n1040\t        array + first_seq are stored. Validated on load against the segment size. MUST hold `_index_lock`.\"\"\"\n1041\t        entries = sorted((seq, off) for seq, (q, off) in self._offsets.items() if q == ps)\n1042\t        if not entries:\n1043\t            return\n1044\t        try:\n1045\t            data = json.dumps({\"v\": 1, \"first_seq\": entries[0][0], \"seg_bytes\": p.stat().st_size,\n1046\t                               \"offsets\": [off for _, off in entries]}, separators=(\",\", \":\"))\n1047\t            atomic_write_text(p.with_name(p.name + \".idx\"), data, prefix=\".idx-\")\n1048\t        except OSError:                                      # read-only FS etc. — the index still works by scanning\n1049\t            pass\n1050\t\n1051\t    def _purge_segment(self, ps: str) -> None:\n1052\t        \"\"\"Drop a segment's offsets (the active shrank in place — a truncation). MUST hold `_index_lock`.\"\"\"\n1053\t        self._offsets = {s: loc for s, loc in self._offsets.items() if loc[0] != ps}\n1054\t        self._seg_scanned.pop(ps, None)\n1055\t        self._max_seq = max(self._offsets, default=-1)\n1056\t\n1057\t    def _scan_segment(self, ps: str, p: Path, start: int, size: int) -> None:\n1058\t        \"\"\"Index every COMPLETE (newline-terminated) record line of segment `p` as\n1059\t        `_offsets[seq] = (ps, line_offset)`. MUST hold `_index_lock`. Plaintext: index bytes [start, size)\n1060\t        (append-only ⇒ extend-forward). Gzip (sealed, immutable): index the whole DECOMPRESSED stream once\n1061\t        (offsets are decompressed byte offsets, matching `gzip.GzipFile.seek()` in get()). A malformed\n1062\t        complete line is left OUT (a mid-file gap still surfaces via verify()); a trailing partial line is\n1063\t        left for the next extend.\"\"\"\n1064\t        if p.suffix == \".gz\":\n1065\t            with gzip.open(p, \"rb\") as gf:\n1066\t                chunk = gf.read()\n1067\t            base = 0                                    # decompressed offsets; a sealed gz is indexed once\n1068\t        else:\n1069\t            if size <= start:\n1070\t                return\n1071\t            with p.open(\"rb\") as f:\n1072\t                f.seek(start)\n1073\t                chunk = f.read(size - start)\n1074\t            base = start\n1075\t        consumed = 0\n1076\t        while True:\n1077\t            nl = chunk.find(b\"\\n\", consumed)\n1078\t            if nl == -1:\n1079\t                break                                       # trailing partial line — not complete yet\n1080\t            raw = chunk[consumed:nl]\n1081\t            line_off = base + consumed\n1082\t            consumed = nl + 1\n1083\t            if not raw.strip():\n1084\t                continue\n1085\t            try:\n1086\t                d = json.loads(raw)\n1087\t                if not all(k in d for k in _REQUIRED_KEYS):\n1088\t                    raise KeyError\n1089\t                seq = d[\"seq\"]\n1090\t            except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n1091\t                continue                                    # corrupt complete line — do not index it\n1092\t            self._offsets[seq] = (ps, line_off)\n1093\t            if seq > self._max_seq:\n1094\t                self._max_seq = seq\n1095\t        self._seg_scanned[ps] = base + consumed\n1096\t"}]},"uuid":"ec79f296-dab4-4287-a683-6c2c0c91e129","timestamp":"2026-07-20T00:40:26.070Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"50c75d8b-408a-4e2b-8ed2-020a33363868","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"6ff3dc7a-933a-4f49-adae-8224f6f1c373","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"440dc55d-7346-4ae6-a6fa-9f9e6bac39c4","agentId":"a8b19cafd020e87a3","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01WcnYcsZMF8QMhQbbfvYkdD","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Cold-archive hard-prune Slice D — the NON-DESTRUCTIVE machinery: Merkle accumulator, snapshot payload,\n2\tarchive COPY (no live drop), §7 referential guards, and the `--with-archive` re-attach verifier.\n3\tPins: a multi-segment spine archives whole sealed segments below K; the re-attach verifier links [archive‖\n4\tlive] from genesis + re-derives every Merkle root; at-rest tamper (a flipped archived byte) is caught;\n5\tthe referential floor refuses a K that would prune an open workflow; K must be segment-aligned; a two-prune\n6\tfold-of-fold snapshot == the single-scan fold. NO live record is deleted here.\n7\tRun: SIGIL_HOME=$(mktemp -d) ~/.sigil/venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_spine_prune_archive.py -q\n8\t\"\"\"\n9\timport json\n10\t\n11\timport pytest\n12\t\n13\timport sigil.spine.checkpoint as _cp\n14\timport sigil.spine.floor as _fl\n15\timport sigil.spine.snapshot as _snap\n16\tfrom sigil.spine import prune\n17\tfrom sigil.spine.merkle import chain_cumulative, merkle_root\n18\tfrom sigil.spine.snapshot import SnapshotState, build\n19\tfrom sigil.spine.store import SpineStore\n20\t\n21\t\n22\t@pytest.fixture\n23\tdef isolated(tmp_path, monkeypatch):\n24\t    \"\"\"Isolate the owner head + keys + floor to per-test paths (the config globals are process-shared, so\n25\t    without this a prior test's floor/head would contaminate verify_checkpoint here).\"\"\"\n26\t    import sigil.config as cfg\n27\t    keys = tmp_path / \"keys\"\n28\t    head = tmp_path / \"head.json\"\n29\t    monkeypatch.setattr(_cp, \"HEAD_PATH\", head)\n30\t    monkeypatch.setattr(_snap, \"HEAD_PATH\", head)\n31\t    monkeypatch.setattr(cfg, \"HEAD_PATH\", head, raising=False)\n32\t    monkeypatch.setattr(_cp, \"KEYS_DIR\", keys)\n33\t    monkeypatch.setattr(_cp, \"_PRIV\", keys / \"owner.priv\")\n34\t    monkeypatch.setattr(_cp, \"_PUB\", keys / \"owner.pub\")\n35\t    monkeypatch.setattr(_fl, \"FLOOR_PATH\", tmp_path / \"floor.json\")\n36\t    return head\n37\t\n38\t\n39\tdef _segmented_store(tmp_path, n_segments=4, per=5):\n40\t    \"\"\"A migrated store rotated into `n_segments` sealed segments (each `per` records) + a live tail.\"\"\"\n41\t    s = SpineStore(tmp_path / \"spine.jsonl\")\n42\t    s.migrate()\n43\t    seq = 0\n44\t    for _seg in range(n_segments):\n45\t        for _i in range(per):\n46\t            s.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"t\", actor=\"u\", payload={\"i\": seq})\n47\t            seq += 1\n48\t        s.rotate()                                          # seal the active, start a fresh one\n49\t    for _i in range(3):                                     # a live (unsealed) tail\n50\t        s.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"t\", actor=\"u\", payload={\"i\": seq})\n51\t        seq += 1\n52\t    return s\n53\t\n54\t\n55\tdef test_merkle_root_properties():\n56\t    assert merkle_root([]) == \"\"\n57\t    assert merkle_root([\"a\"]) != \"\"\n58\t    # count-sensitive (dup-last-on-odd + committed count): 2 leaves != 3 leaves\n59\t    assert merkle_root([\"a\", \"b\"]) != merkle_root([\"a\", \"b\", \"c\"])\n60\t    # order-sensitive\n61\t    assert merkle_root([\"a\", \"b\", \"c\"]) != merkle_root([\"c\", \"b\", \"a\"])\n62\t    # cumulative chains + is distinct from a bare delta\n63\t    d0 = merkle_root([\"a\", \"b\"])\n64\t    c0 = chain_cumulative(\"\", d0)\n65\t    assert c0 != d0 and chain_cumulative(c0, merkle_root([\"c\"])) != c0\n66\t\n67\t\n68\tdef test_archive_copy_and_reattach_verify(tmp_path, isolated):\n69\t    s = _segmented_store(tmp_path)\n70\t    _cp.checkpoint(s)                                       # owner-sign the live spine (the verifier's anchor)\n71\t    K = 10                                                  # a sealed-segment boundary (segments start 0,5,10,15)\n72\t    payload = prune.snapshot_payload(s, K)\n73\t    assert payload[\"base_seq\"] == 10 and payload[\"base_count\"] == 10\n74\t    rep = prune.archive_copy(s, K, payload, adir=tmp_path / \"arch\")\n75\t    assert rep[\"verified\"] and rep[\"copied\"] == 2          # segments [0..5) and [5..10)\n76\t    # NO live drop: the store still holds every record.\n77\t    assert s.count() == 23 and s.verify()[0]\n78\t    ok, msg = prune.verify_with_archive(s, adir=tmp_path / \"arch\")\n79\t    assert ok, msg\n80\t    # the OWNER anchor is load-bearing: an UNSIGNED spine cannot certify the archive.\n81\t    (tmp_path / \"head.json\").unlink()\n82\t    ok2, msg2 = prune.verify_with_archive(s, adir=tmp_path / \"arch\")\n83\t    assert not ok2 and \"does not verify\" in msg2\n84\t\n85\t\n86\tdef test_reattach_detects_at_rest_tamper(tmp_path):\n87\t    s = _segmented_store(tmp_path)\n88\t    payload = prune.snapshot_payload(s, 10)\n89\t    prune.archive_copy(s, 10, payload, adir=tmp_path / \"arch\")\n90\t    # flip a byte in an archived segment file -> sha256 mismatch caught.\n91\t    seg0 = next((tmp_path / \"arch\" / \"segments\").glob(\"seg-*\"))\n92\t    raw = seg0.read_bytes()\n93\t    seg0.write_bytes(raw[:-2] + b\"X\\n\")\n94\t    ok, msg = prune.verify_with_archive(s, adir=tmp_path / \"arch\")\n95\t    assert not ok and \"sha256\" in msg.lower()\n96\t\n97\t\n98\tdef test_payload_forged_archive_is_rejected(tmp_path, isolated):\n99\t    \"\"\"Review HIGH (binding) + BLOCK (anchor): rewriting a pruned record's PAYLOAD while keeping the chain\n100\t    fields must be caught — by the per-record content binding and/or the cross-check against the owner-signed\n101\t    live spine. A self-consistent sha256 in the manifest does NOT save it.\"\"\"\n102\t    import hashlib\n103\t\n104\t    s = _segmented_store(tmp_path)\n105\t    _cp.checkpoint(s)\n106\t    payload = prune.snapshot_payload(s, 10)\n107\t    prune.archive_copy(s, 10, payload, adir=tmp_path / \"arch\")\n108\t    assert prune.verify_with_archive(s, adir=tmp_path / \"arch\")[0]\n109\t\n110\t    seg = sorted((tmp_path / \"arch\" / \"segments\").glob(\"seg-*\"))[0]\n111\t    seg.write_text(seg.read_text().replace('\"i\": 0', '\"i\": 999'))   # forge a payload; cert_digest now stale\n112\t    mp = tmp_path / \"arch\" / \"archive.manifest.json\"\n113\t    am = json.loads(mp.read_text())\n114\t    for row in am[\"segments\"]:                                       # make sha256 self-consistent with the forgery\n115\t        am_p = tmp_path / \"arch\" / row[\"file\"]\n116\t        row[\"sha256\"] = hashlib.sha256(am_p.read_bytes()).hexdigest()\n117\t    mp.write_text(json.dumps(am))\n118\t    ok, msg = prune.verify_with_archive(s, adir=tmp_path / \"arch\")\n119\t    assert not ok, \"a payload-forged archive with a self-consistent sha256 must be rejected\"\n120\t\n121\t\n122\tdef test_open_workflow_floor_counts_unexecuted_operator_plan(tmp_path):\n123\t    \"\"\"Review HIGH: an un-executed operator.plan is an open loop — the floor must sit at (or below) it, so a\n124\t    prune can't delete the plan + its preview.\"\"\"\n125\t    s = _segmented_store(tmp_path)\n126\t    plan_seq = s.append(kind=\"operation\", source=\"operator\", actor=\"OPERATOR\",\n127\t                        payload={\"signal\": \"operator.plan\", \"subject\": \"x\", \"decision\": \"queued\"})\n128\t    assert prune.open_workflow_floor(s) <= plan_seq\n129\t    # once executed, it no longer floors the prune.\n130\t    s.append(kind=\"operation\", source=\"operator\", actor=\"OPERATOR\",\n131\t             payload={\"signal\": \"operator.execute\", \"target_seq\": plan_seq, \"status\": \"APPLIED\"})\n132\t    assert prune.open_workflow_floor(s) > plan_seq\n133\t\n134\t\n135\tdef test_referential_floor_blocks_open_workflow(tmp_path, monkeypatch):\n136\t    s = _segmented_store(tmp_path)\n137\t    # force an open-workflow floor at seq 3 (inside the first segment) -> a prune at K=10 must be refused.\n138\t    monkeypatch.setattr(prune, \"open_workflow_floor\", lambda store: 3)\n139\t    with pytest.raises(prune.PruneUnsafe) as e:\n140\t        prune.check_prune_safe(s, 10)\n141\t    assert \"referential floor\" in str(e.value)\n142\t\n143\t\n144\tdef test_k_must_be_segment_aligned(tmp_path):\n145\t    s = _segmented_store(tmp_path)\n146\t    with pytest.raises(prune.PruneUnsafe) as e:\n147\t        prune.check_prune_safe(s, 7)                        # 7 is mid-segment, not a boundary\n148\t    assert \"boundary\" in str(e.value)\n149\t\n150\t\n151\tdef test_two_prune_fold_of_fold_equals_single_scan(tmp_path):\n152\t    \"\"\"Multi-prune: build(prior_folded, delta[K1..K2)) == build(empty, [0..K2)). The snapshot's folded_state\n153\t    accumulator is exact across successive prunes.\"\"\"\n154\t    s = _segmented_store(tmp_path)\n155\t    all_recs = list(s.iter_records())\n156\t    # prune #1 at K1=5, prune #2 at K2=10 seeded from #1.\n157\t    p1 = prune.snapshot_payload(s, 5)\n158\t    p2 = prune.snapshot_payload(s, 10, prior=p1)\n159\t    folded_2 = SnapshotState.model_validate(p2[\"folded_state\"])\n160\t    single = build([r for r in all_recs if r.seq < 10], trusted_pubkey=p2[\"trusted_pubkey\"],\n161\t                   base_seq=10, snapshot_seq=-1)\n162\t    assert folded_2.model_dump() == single.model_dump()    # fold-of-fold == single scan\n163\t    # and the cumulative Merkle chains the two deltas\n164\t    assert p2[\"cumulative_merkle_root\"] == chain_cumulative(p1[\"cumulative_merkle_root\"], p2[\"delta_merkle_root\"])\n165\t\n166\t\n167\tdef test_snapshot_payload_is_json_and_kind_registered(tmp_path):\n168\t    from sigil.spine.models import KINDS\n169\t    assert \"snapshot\" in KINDS\n170\t    s = _segmented_store(tmp_path)\n171\t    payload = prune.snapshot_payload(s, 10)\n172\t    json.dumps(payload)                                     # fully JSON-serializable\n173\t    # appending it is a normal retain-all append (inert until a head commits snapshot_seq — Slice E)\n174\t    seq = s.append(kind=\"snapshot\", source=\"spine\", actor=\"OWNER\", payload=payload)\n175\t    assert s.get(seq).kind == \"snapshot\" and s.verify()[0]\n176\t"}]},"uuid":"d55296fc-af51-4925-89e8-3601ed641d74","timestamp":"2026-07-20T00:40:31.536Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"6ff3dc7a-933a-4f49-adae-8224f6f1c373","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"4ceba695-a847-4561-b300-5a8daf744ef9","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"440dc55d-7346-4ae6-a6fa-9f9e6bac39c4","agentId":"a8b19cafd020e87a3","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01VUyahf9w46mM5yuW3585EG","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Approval queue (SIGIL §5, §9.3 mobile bridge) — the human gate for A2/A3 proposals. An agent\n2\tQUEUES a proposal (governor → QUEUE); the owner APPROVES or DENIES it here. Authentication is an\n3\tEd25519 signature over the canonical (target, decision, approver) by the OWNER key.\n4\t\n5\tRED-PEN HARDENING (Phase 6):\n6\t • The trusted pubkey is the PERSISTED owner identity — NOT the key supplied to the queue, so an\n7\t   attacker cannot self-certify by bringing their own key (RP-APPROVAL-2). approve/deny require the\n8\t   supplied signing key to BE the trusted owner key.\n9\t • `pending()` treats a queued item as resolved ONLY when a superseding approval VERIFIES against the\n10\t   trusted owner key, and keys resolution off the SIGNED target_seq (not the raw supersedes_id) — so\n11\t   an unsigned/forged approval can't silently drop an item (RP-1/RP-APPROVAL-1), and a genuine\n12\t   approval of one item can't be replayed onto another (RP-APPROVAL-4). `verify_approval` is thereby\n13\t   on the enforcement path (RP-APPROVAL-3).\n14\t\n15\tThis records the DECISION; it executes no external effect (SIGIL has no send/deploy path). The\n16\ttransport (Telegram/WhatsApp over WireGuard, §9.3) is a seam over this authenticated core.\"\"\"\n17\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n18\t\n19\tfrom typing import List, Optional\n20\t\n21\tfrom ..reuse import canonical_json, sha256_hex, sign, verify_one\n22\tfrom ..spine.store import SpineStore\n23\t\n24\tSIGNAL = \"governor.approval\"\n25\t_QUEUED_STATUS = \"awaiting-approval\"\n26\t\n27\t\n28\tdef _approval_message(target_seq, decision: str, approver: str) -> bytes:\n29\t    m = canonical_json({\"target\": target_seq, \"decision\": decision, \"approver\": approver})\n30\t    return m if isinstance(m, bytes) else m.encode()\n31\t\n32\t\n33\tdef verify_approval(record, trusted_pubkey_b64: Optional[str], *, extra_pubkeys=None) -> bool:\n34\t    \"\"\"True iff the approval carries a valid signature over its (target, decision, approver) by the\n35\t    OWNER key OR by a currently-authorized DEVICE key (`extra_pubkeys`). The claimed `pubkey` must be\n36\t    in the allowed set — which is composed ONLY of owner-minted keys (the owner identity + the\n37\t    owner-signed device-authorization ledger, see `sigil.mesh`) — so a key the requester merely\n38\t    presents is never trusted (RP-APPROVAL-2). The signed `target_seq` still binds (RP-APPROVAL-4).\n39\t    Unsigned / not-in-allowed-set / no-trusted-key → False (fail-closed).\"\"\"\n40\t    p = record.payload if hasattr(record, \"payload\") else record\n41\t    sig, pub = p.get(\"sig\"), p.get(\"pubkey\")\n42\t    allowed = set()\n43\t    if trusted_pubkey_b64:\n44\t        allowed.add(trusted_pubkey_b64)\n45\t    if extra_pubkeys:\n46\t        allowed.update(extra_pubkeys)\n47\t    if not sig or not pub or pub not in allowed:\n48\t        return False\n49\t    msg = _approval_message(p.get(\"target_seq\"), p.get(\"approval\"), p.get(\"approver\"))\n50\t    return verify_one(pub, msg, sig)                # verify against the (owner-minted) claimed key\n51\t\n52\t\n53\tdef pending(store: SpineStore, trusted_pubkey_b64: Optional[str] = None, *, extra_pubkeys=None) -> List:\n54\t    \"\"\"Queued proposals with NO valid approval yet, oldest first. Resolution requires a VERIFIED\n55\t    owner/authorized-device approval; an unsigned/forged/unauthorized approval leaves the item\n56\t    pending (fail-closed).\n57\t\n58\t    Hard-prune fold (SnapshotState §approvals): seed empty and window the LIVE records `[base_seq..T]`\n59\t    only. The RESOLVED set is query-pubkey-dependent (it turns on `trusted_pubkey_b64`/`extra_pubkeys`),\n60\t    so the snapshot deliberately carries NO resolved/queued seed — it carries only the referential-floor\n61\t    ASSERT (`open_queued_below_base`, expected EMPTY: a prune must never orphan an OPEN queued item below\n62\t    the floor). Because nothing pubkey-dependent is seeded from the snapshot (the resolved set is always\n63\t    recomputed fresh over the live window with the caller's key), no trusted-pubkey bypass is needed.\n64\t    Under Slice-C's empty snapshot `base_seq==0` ⇒ `since_seq=-1` (the current full genesis scan) with\n65\t    empty seeds and the assert a no-op ([]), so this is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the prior full scan.\"\"\"\n66\t    if trusted_pubkey_b64 is None:\n67\t        from ..governor.identity import owner_pubkey\n68\t        trusted_pubkey_b64 = owner_pubkey()\n69\t    from ..spine.snapshot import SnapshotState\n70\t    st = SnapshotState.load(store)\n71\t    if st.open_queued_below_base:\n72\t        # referential-floor ASSERT violated: the pruned prefix left OPEN queued item(s) below the floor,\n73\t        # which this truncated window can no longer see. Fail closed (as SnapshotState.load does) rather\n74\t        # than serve a pending list that silently drops them. Never reached under the empty snapshot ([]).\n75\t        raise ApprovalError(\n76\t            f\"snapshot floor at base_seq {st.base_seq} carries {len(st.open_queued_below_base)} open \"\n77\t            f\"queued item(s) below the prune floor — refusing to serve a truncated pending() window\")\n78\t    resolved = set()\n79\t    queued = {}\n80\t    for r in store.iter_records(since_seq=st.base_seq - 1):\n81\t        p = r.payload\n82\t        if p.get(\"signal\") == SIGNAL and verify_approval(r, trusted_pubkey_b64, extra_pubkeys=extra_pubkeys):\n83\t            resolved.add(p.get(\"target_seq\"))       # honor the SIGNED target, not raw supersedes_id\n84\t        if p.get(\"decision\") == \"queued\" and p.get(\"status\") == _QUEUED_STATUS:\n85\t            queued[r.seq] = r\n86\t    return [r for seq, r in sorted(queued.items()) if seq not in resolved]\n87\t\n88\t\n89\tclass ApprovalError(Exception):\n90\t    pass\n91\t\n92\t\n93\tclass ApprovalQueue:\n94\t    def __init__(self, store: Optional[SpineStore] = None, *, owner_key=None,\n95\t                 trusted_pubkey_b64: Optional[str] = None):\n96\t        from ..governor.identity import owner_keypair, owner_pubkey\n97\t        self.store = store or SpineStore()\n98\t        self.owner_key = owner_key if owner_key is not None else owner_keypair()\n99\t        # PINNED to the persisted owner identity — never defaulted to the supplied key (RP-APPROVAL-2)\n100\t        self.trusted_pubkey_b64 = trusted_pubkey_b64 if trusted_pubkey_b64 is not None else owner_pubkey()\n101\t\n102\t    def _target(self, seq: int):\n103\t        for r in pending(self.store, self.trusted_pubkey_b64):\n104\t            if r.seq == seq:\n105\t                return r\n106\t        raise ApprovalError(f\"seq {seq} is not a pending approval (already decided, or not queued)\")\n107\t\n108\t    def _decide(self, seq: int, decision: str, *, approver: str, reason: str = \"\") -> int:\n109\t        target = self._target(seq)\n110\t        # the signing key MUST be the trusted owner key — not merely \"some key present\" (RP-APPROVAL-2).\n111\t        if (self.owner_key is None or self.trusted_pubkey_b64 is None\n112\t                or self.owner_key.public_key_b64 != self.trusted_pubkey_b64):\n113\t            raise ApprovalError(\"approval requires the OWNER's signing key (missing or not the trusted key)\")\n114\t        msg = _approval_message(seq, decision, approver)\n115\t        sig = sign(self.owner_key.private_key_b64, msg)\n116\t        payload = {\"signal\": SIGNAL, \"approval\": decision, \"target_seq\": seq, \"target_kind\": target.kind,\n117\t                   \"target_tier\": target.payload.get(\"tier\"), \"approver\": approver, \"reason\": reason,\n118\t                   \"pubkey\": self.trusted_pubkey_b64, \"sig\": sig, \"msg_digest\": sha256_hex(msg),\n119\t                   \"tier\": \"A0\", \"decision\": \"auto\"}\n120\t        return self.store.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"governor\", actor=\"OWNER\",\n121\t                                 payload=payload, supersedes_id=seq)\n122\t\n123\t    def approve(self, seq: int, *, approver: str = \"owner\", reason: str = \"\") -> int:\n124\t        return self._decide(seq, \"approved\", approver=approver, reason=reason)\n125\t\n126\t    def deny(self, seq: int, *, approver: str = \"owner\", reason: str = \"\") -> int:\n127\t        return self._decide(seq, \"denied\", approver=approver, reason=reason)\n128\t"}]},"uuid":"9eca7125-83d0-469e-8657-837c0997771a","timestamp":"2026-07-20T00:40:31.830Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"4ceba695-a847-4561-b300-5a8daf744ef9","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"317dbe6d-5652-442a-92f1-dd1f6f2b4b23","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"440dc55d-7346-4ae6-a6fa-9f9e6bac39c4","agentId":"a8b19cafd020e87a3","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01C1L69tqsrAcowm8ypgJHbb","type":"tool_result","content":"diff --git a/sigil/spine/prune.py b/sigil/spine/prune.py\nindex 45172f1..049a4a5 100644\n--- a/sigil/spine/prune.py\n+++ b/sigil/spine/prune.py\n@@ -7,11 +7,14 @@ The archive is `SIGIL_HOME/spine/archive/` (relocatable via `SIGIL_SPINE_ARCHIVE\n     archive/segments/seg-*.jsonl[.gz]     # whole sealed segments, byte-identical (copy, never a rename)\n     archive/archive.manifest.json         # APPEND-ONLY across prunes: one row per archived segment + snapshot\n \n-Doctrine: MOVE = copy → verify → (Slice E) drop; here we stop after copy+verify (additive-only). A verifier\n-handed the archive re-attaches [archive ‖ live] into one genesis-rooted stream, verify_chain()s it, and\n-re-derives every delta_merkle_root from the REAL archived leaves — proving the pruned prefix is exactly what\n-the signed snapshots committed. Losing the archive makes the pruned prefix unrecoverable (only the Merkle\n-commitment survives) — an explicit MAX-RECLAIM tradeoff.\n+Doctrine: MOVE = copy → verify → (Slice E) drop; here we stop after copy+verify (additive-only). The\n+verifier (`verify_with_archive`) anchors the archive to the OWNER SIGNATURE — never to the (attacker-\n+controllable) archive manifest: it requires a valid owner-signed live head, checks the per-record content\n+binding + the genesis chain, and ties the archived leaves to the owner key (before a prune commits: the\n+archive must equal the still-present live prefix; after: the archive tail == the signed head.base_prev_hash\n+and the re-derived cumulative == the signed head.cumulative_merkle_root). Only then is the manifest's own\n+Merkle re-derivation a meaningful self-consistency cross-check.
